Whatever the chain does not show leaves the whole gap open, written as unwatched from its start to the first current heartbeat after its cause ends that shows capture. So any sensor restart inside a gap leaves the whole gap open, with that cause. For a break longer than `backlog_tie_s`, **this is D3's condition, adopted by D1 (comment 6837169700 on #641) as a refinement of D4-90:** "covered" must be shown, and across such a restart it cannot be. Extending it to shorter breaks is #644's proposal, which fails closed, and D3 accepts it. D4-90's sentence that an interval is closed once its backlog is through holds wherever the chain shows coverage.
    - **Three causes are not closed by a chain** (`records-auditor` on #641):
      - **A `lost` gap is its span:** from the stated `from_utc` to the fixed cut end (CH-40, SL-31). It is written once with those times, and no heartbeat makes it covered. **Its times are the one exception to "on the node's clock"** (D6's N7 on #641 at `9d74bb73`): its start is the sensor's stated time, and only its end is bounded by the node's clock, at the receipt time plus the skew bound. Within the skew bound the span sits within 2,000 ms of where it fell; with a sensor clock outside it, `sensor-clock-skew` stands, and the span can be displaced by up to the offset, so the own-session check is off for an interval shifted from the one lost. The cut end still keeps the span from reaching past the node's own bound. Whether a span is brought to the node's clock by E, as a backlog record's time is (CH-39), is D4's to state in CH-40; D3 asks for it there, since it narrows the shift to E's own error.
      - **A `blind` gap closes unwatched** (`covered: false`) at the first current heartbeat in which an interface's `packets` rose (CH-31). *Test:* gap leg 17's second case (D6's N3 on #641 at `9d74bb73`).
      - **A `revoked` gap is closed only by the first current heartbeat, showing capture, of a run under a later enrolment's generation.** That is a new key, since a revoked pin is never enrolled again (SL-R4). It closes unwatched (`covered: false`), never as covered: no chain runs across a new generation's run 1, and nothing the revoked key sent or would have sent is trusted for that interval. Nothing else closes it: not a restart, a change of configuration, or time. With no later enrolment it stays open, and so does its last record in the stream (posted first as comment 6838033945 on #641).
    - **Names that are D5's.** The event's detail names, among them `from_bound` (a start that is a bound) and `clock_stepped` (a clock stepped back across the gap), and the field `enrolled_at` in `sensors.json` are D5's to name. This note states only what each must carry.
  - A heartbeat is still the sensor's word. These conditions stop an honest fault from closing a gap. They do not stop a compromised sensor from claiming capture it did not make (AP-36).
  - *Test:* the gap legs of C8-7.
- **Residual.** A copy that always moves its counter forward is accepted until the original shows up. Against a stolen key only revocation helps (§6). A TPM-held key removes copies made from a disk or an image (O-2). A sensor that is a virtual machine can be copied by a snapshot (O-9).
- *Test:* SL-T9.

**SL-17. Time and late records: constraints for P4-4-L2 and P4-4-L3.** The bounds are theirs to set. D3's constraints:

- The node never orders, prunes or expires anything by the sensor's clock. It adds its own received time, as today.
- A sensor clock outside the skew bound raises the skew alarm. While it is outside, a tie between an observation and one of the node's own sessions (AP-35) is not made: the flow is judged untied, which alerts. Skew must never suppress. Every scheduled session then alerts for as long as the skew lasts, so those alerts are aggregated, with the skew alarm naming the cause, and they do not fill the alert class (D5 on #624; how is P4-4-L3's).
- A backlog after an outage may delay an alert, and never suppresses one. The hold for the node's own sessions stays bounded while it waits for a backlog, as AP-35 requires today.
- The node does not serve time to the sensor. That would be an inbound port on the node (SL-18) and a second channel to the sensor (SL-19).
- **How C13 0.2 and the node's design meet these** (CH-22, CH-23 and CH-39 to CH-41; #628 §1.5 and §1.6; D1's ruling 1 on #627 and its ruling on round two):
  - **The alarm** is judged on E, the least offset of the last six ticks, against a bound of 2,000 ms. **The tie reads the latest sample too:** no tie is made while the alarm stands, or while the latest sample is outside the bound.
  - **A tie on a backlog record fails closed.** The node bounds a backlog record's clock error only by what it measured itself, the length of the break on its own clock. After a break longer than `backlog_tie_s` (60 s, at most 120 s), a record numbered while no connection was open takes no tie, and the own-session alerts of that outage are aggregated into one alert that names its assets up to a cap. After a shorter break, such a record ties only when the time the agent numbered it, brought to the node's clock, falls inside the break.
  - **A step of the node's clock inside a short break gives no tie** (D3's second sweep, for D6's B1 on #641 at `6723bf90`). The break's length is on the monotonic clock (CH-39), but the window that `made_utc` + E must fall in runs between its two ends on the wall clock. A step left inside the break puts those ends on two clocks, while E, taken on the new connection, puts the record on the new one. **The case needs the sensor's clock and the node's to agree on the new connection** (D6's B2 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`, which corrects this note's example there). E is per connection, the hello's o until the first tick (CH-22), so with the node's clock stepped alone, E on the new connection is about the step, and CH-22 already makes no tie. Say a 40 s break, and one time source serving both hosts steps both clocks back 30 s at 10 s into it: the window is the wall-clock times of 0 s to 10 s into the break; a record made 35 s into it carries a `made_utc` of 5 s, E on the new connection is about 0, and the record is placed at 5 s, inside the window, where it can tie to a session that ran at 5 s, 30 s before the flow. That suppresses an alert. (The node's clock run 30 s ahead of the sensor's before the break, with the alarm standing, and stepped back into agreement 10 s into it, does the same.) **So a break whose length on the wall clock differs from its length on the monotonic clock by more than `max_skew_ms` is judged as longer than `backlog_tie_s`:** no backlog record of it takes a tie, and its own-session alerts are aggregated. #644 keeps the wall and monotonic times of both ends already (§1.6), so the rule adds no field. It fails closed: with no step the two lengths agree, and with one no tie is made. **It is the next bullet's first half applied to a break's two ends,** which are stored times like any other, and it stays in these words because #644 §1.6 states it so. *Test:* that break, with `tie_grace_s` at 1 s, an own session (a dial that fails, so the session enters the late index) that ran 5 s into it, and a backlog record made 35 s into it of a flow from the node's address to the session's relay, stamped 5 s by the stepped source: the record is committed untied with `node.backlog: true`, with no `node.skewed`, and alerts under `sensor-records-late` (the capture floor refuses it too, and CH-39 judges it: next bullet). *Control:* a node with no rule on the two clocks (next bullet), which judges the break by its monotonic length alone and compares stored times as they are, ties it to the session.
  - **No tie across a move of either clock** (D6's B1 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`, D6's fix, which D1 accepted, with a second half added by D3; D5's point on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`; the second half keyed on capture time for D6's B1 on #641 at `50a67730`). The tie compares `t_sensor + E` with times stored on the node's wall clock: `Result`'s window, part (d)'s expiry, the late index, and a break's two ends for the `made_utc` check. Nothing stored is re-timed (#644 §1.5). CH-22 compares the sensor's clock with the node's, so it cannot see a step that one time source makes on both clocks, nor a correction of the node's clock alone once the alarm has stood: after either, the two agree. **The cases** (D6's B1): a dial to a relay that fails is recorded as [10:00:00, 10:00:10], so its session enters the late index; at 10:00:30 a source serving both hosts steps both clocks back 300 s; the node's step check moves E's held samples back with it, so `sensor-clock-skew` stands for about a minute, until they leave the six ticks, and then clears; at 10:05:00 a Telnet session from the node's address to that relay is stamped 10:00:00, placed at 10:00:00 + E and tied to the run, with no alert. A node clock that ran 300 s ahead and is corrected does the same for every run recorded during the error. Steps under `max_skew_ms` add up the same way. The displacement is the step, which whoever controls the source chooses. **The rule has two halves, one for each side of the comparison:**
    - **A stored time ties only while the node's clock has not moved since the node recorded it.** With each time a tie may compare, the node keeps the offset of its wall clock from its monotonic clock at the moment it recorded that time, and compares a sensor time with it only while the current offset is within `max_skew_ms` of that one. **The offsets are compared end to end, not by the steps found,** so steps under the threshold that add up are caught. A run's window is the collector's `started_at` and `ended_at`, which the collector reads on the same host's clock and the node does not see read: so the node keeps its offset at the run's `Dispatch` and at its `Result`, both must agree with the current one, and the two times must lie between the node's own wall-clock times at those two moments, within `max_skew_ms`. **A time recorded before the node's start ties nothing,** as the first break after a start counts as long: so the late index rebuilt at a start (#644 §1.6) and the open-flow table read at a start tie nothing. D5 drops the rebuild (D5 on #641 at `50a67730`). **These refusals have no clock behind them** (D6's N3 on #641 at `50a67730`): their flows alert under `clock-skew`, aggregated, and status counts them apart as refused for a node start, not as a clock move (the name is D5's). A break's two ends are stored times too: the short-break rule above is this half's case for a step inside the break, and **D5's case is its case for a step after the break:** a step found after the hello while the backlog is read leaves the break's ends on the old clock, so no record of that backlog ties. D5 asked for a rule of its own, that a step found while a backlog is unfinished makes the backlog judged as longer than `backlog_tie_s`; this half gives the same outcome, record by record, with no rule of its own.
    - **A record ties only if it was captured after the last move of the sensor's clock against the node's monotonic clock** (D6's B1 on #641 at `50a67730`, D6's fix (a), which D1 preferred, in the form below). A placed time is right on the node's current clock only while the sensor's clock holds the offset from the node's monotonic clock that it had at capture. A step of the node's clock alone moves E with it, and placed times stay right; a step of the sensor's clock, alone or with the node's, displaces every record captured before it, however late the record is written. The first half sees neither once the two clocks agree again.
      - **K.** At each tick that the node takes a sample from, and at each hello, the node takes K, the `sensor_utc` it states less the node's monotonic time at its receipt. A tick counted under `tick_early` does no further work (CH-8) and gives no K (D6's B2 on #641 at `e164e416`). K's baseline is its value at the first hello after the node's start, or the K just taken at the last change (D5's point on #641 at `e164e416`), **but never a K read after a held read loop** (#644 §1.5 item 6; D6's N4 on #641 at `e164e416`): such a K can raise the floor (next), and the baseline stays where it was. **K and its baseline are kept for each sensor across its connections while the node runs,** since the monotonic clock runs on across a break: a move made inside a break is then seen at the next hello. Kept for each connection, as at `50a67730`, it was not, and a flow captured before the break and written after the hello tied, displaced by the move, with no rule to stop it: the backlog's rules judge only what is numbered before the hello's `newest_seq`.
      - **The capture floor.** When K differs from its baseline by more than `max_skew_ms`, the node raises the capture floor to the highest K taken from the baseline on, the baseline included and the K that shows the change excluded, plus the node's monotonic time at the receipt of the tick or hello that shows the change, plus `max_skew_ms`: the old clock's reading at that receipt, as the node last measured it, with the bound added. (With the K that shows the change included, a step forward would raise the floor to the new clock's reading: D6's N5 on #641 at `e164e416`.) The K just taken becomes the baseline, unless it was read after a held loop (above). **The floor never falls,** while the node runs or across a start (next): a value below the one held keeps the one held. **No record whose capture time is at or before the floor takes a tie,** whenever it was numbered and whenever it is read. It is one value for each sensor. **It is not SL-16's gap floor** (D6's N4 on #641 at `94834c5a`): the step rule never moves the capture floor, M or the start bound back and never caps them at the node's wall clock, since each is a reading of the sensor's clock. Applied to them, a step of both clocks seen while the node runs would move the floor back by the step, and the cap at a start after a step during the stop would take it down to the new clock, and either would tie what the floor refuses. **Status shows the capture floor's value for each sensor, and in C13 0.2 no one can lower it** (D1's ruling on D6's N3 on #641 at `94834c5a`).
      - **The floor across a node start** (D6's B1 on #641 at `e164e416`; D1's ruling on this residual, withdrawn in comment 6845707985, with D1's preference for D6's signal 2 and for signal 1 only where signal 2 leaves a case). K and its baseline do not survive a start, since the monotonic clock does not. The floor does: it is a reading of the sensor's clock, not of the node's. Without it, a floor raised by a step back made while the node ran was lost at the next start, so a flow held open across any later stop and written after the first hello tied, displaced by a step the attacker chooses (D6's route). Two things carry it, and neither needs the node to have seen the move:
        - **M, the sensor's clock as last read, kept in the cursor.** With each cursor write the node saves M: the later of the floor and, once a K has been taken since the start, the highest K from the baseline on plus the node's monotonic time at the write. A change that raises the floor is saved at once, as a step back of the node's clock is (SL-16). At a start the floor is the M saved, and the node holds a **start bound** of M plus `HeartbeatGapMax` plus `max_skew_ms`, in memory only, so that M does not climb at each start. M is one time: 21 bytes in #644 §1.4's form, 2,026 bytes at the largest, 2,032 with `unclean` in full, under a `MaxLinkCursorBytes` of 4,096 (D1's ruling on #645, comment 6846045180; 2,048 before it).
        - **The heartbeats read after the start, up to the end of the backlog** (D6's B1 on #641 at `94834c5a`, D6's fix). Each heartbeat the node reads after the start, in any run and on any connection, up to the `newest_seq` of the first hello after the start whose `current` is true (CH-39's end of the backlog), raises the floor to its `at_utc` plus `HeartbeatGapMax` plus `max_skew_ms`. At `94834c5a` only the heartbeats of the first hello's run raised it, and that missed a second run: the agent offers the oldest run that still holds records above `taken`, one run on each connection (CH-16), so where the agent started a new run during the stop, the first hello is the old run's, with `current: false`, and the new run's heartbeats, which hold the old clock's last reading before a step made during the stop, raised nothing. The runs drained before the current one are backlog and take no tie already, so this costs nothing more.
        - **The spans the sensor states after the start** (D6's N1 on #641 at `94834c5a`, D6's fix, widened). Each span of lost records stated in a message the node reads after the start, before or after that hello, `prior.lost` included, raises the floor to the span's upper end as stated, its latest `last_utc` or `at_utc` (CH-40), plus `HeartbeatGapMax` plus `max_skew_ms`. Heartbeats are evicted like any other record (CH-39, the heartbeat's cadence), so a step back inside a span leaves the old clock's last reading in the span's upper end, within one heartbeat interval. **Why after the hello too:** the agent can go on evicting while the node drains a full buffer, and AP-35's attacker can keep it full with traffic on the monitored network (a site's buffer floor is 256 MiB, CH-20), so a span stated after the hello can hold that reading. A sensor that states a span ending far ahead raises the floor as far, which refuses its own ties and gains it nothing that its `sensor_utc` does not give it already (AP-36). Neither these heartbeats nor the spans add a field: the spans are in the cursor already (SL-31).

        **No record whose capture time is at or before the floor or the start bound takes a tie.** *Why it holds:* a capture made before M's write lies at or before the clock as M last read it, within the start bound (M, like K, understates the clock by a tick's delay in the stream), or, where a move the node saw lies between the capture and the write, at or before the floor, which M carries. A resume resends every record above `taken` (#644 §1.4), heartbeats included, and M is written with `taken`; the agent offers each run that still holds records above it, oldest first and one on each connection, up to its current run (CH-16). So after the write the node reads the sensor's clock heartbeat by heartbeat, across every run, up to the first hello whose `current` is true, but for the heartbeats the agent evicted, whose clock's last reading is the upper end of the span that states them. A capture with no step before the next heartbeat lies at or before that heartbeat's `at_utc`. A capture with a step before the next heartbeat lies within `HeartbeatGapMax` of the last heartbeat made before the step, which is read after the start, or lies in a stated span, or is at or before `taken` and so within M; only a stall of the heartbeats breaks this (below). Those runs' records, and the current run's up to its hello, are read before any record numbered after that hello. Every tick carries the run's full set of spans (CH-40), and states it as synced (CH-8). **The agent syncs the run's `drops_channel` and `lost` before it writes each tick and states them from that sync** (a clause for CH-19; D6's N1 on #641 at `71fde2ac`, which D1 ruled is taken now), so a tick states every eviction made before it is written. Without that sync a tick could state spans up to `buffer_sync_ms` behind the evictions, and an eviction burst across more than about 22 s of quiet capture could leave a tie decided before its raise. The stream is in `seq` order, so a record never sent and numbered before a tied one was evicted before the tied one was sent, and the first tick read after the tied record states it. **A message's stated spans raise the floor before any tie decided after that message is read,** and D5's condition on #644 (comment 6846713504), that a tick's spans are the set as of its writing, holds. **A tie is decided at its record's read (below), so a span first stated in the tick after that record has not raised the floor when the tie is decided:** that is the one-tick residual, stated below with its size and what it takes (D3's ruling on D6's B1 on #644 at `bc19358e`).

        **Why not D6's signal 2 as written** (a floor at a start of W's heartbeat's `at_utc` plus the save interval, a heartbeat interval, `late_bound_s` and `max_skew_ms`). It leaves two cases: a step back seen while the node ran, before the stop, since W's heartbeat is then on the new clock and the bound falls short of the old one by the step less the bound (D6's own case, with Δ = 600 s); and a capture made during the stop, or while a gap held W at the stop, with a step after it, since signal 2 bounds captures before the stop only and W does not move past an open gap. The first case needs a field, so D1's condition for signal 1 is met. With that field read as M, signal 2 adds nothing: M bounds what was captured before its write without `late_bound_s`, whose 3,600 s at the largest would refuse ties for up to an hour after a short stop, and the first break's heartbeats bound what came after. **The host's boot-relative clock (D6's signal 3) is not used:** M and the heartbeats cover a restart of the process alone too.

        **What it costs.** At a start, ties of captures up to the start bound, and up to the last heartbeat read before the end of the backlog plus `HeartbeatGapMax` and `max_skew_ms`, are refused: about the captures of the first 32 s after the first current hello, after a long stop. **After a second start soon after a first hello it is about 64 s** (D6's N3 on #641 at `94834c5a`): captures up to about 64 s past the earlier break's last heartbeat, that heartbeat's 32 s, which M saves, plus the start bound's 32 s; it climbs no further. Those refusals are counted apart as refused for a node start. While the node runs, a stated span refuses ties of captures up to about 32 s past its latest record, after a loss that is already a gap and an alarm (SL-16). **Under sustained eviction the span's upper end follows the eviction front** (D6's N2 on #641 at `71fde2ac`): where the records not yet acknowledged span less than about 32 s of capture, the floor sits at or ahead of live captures, and every tie of that sensor is refused for the length of the flood plus about 32 s. Status counts these refusals apart, as refused for a stated span, not as a clock move (the name is D5's). After a step back of Δ seen before the stop, the saved floor refuses for about Δ + `max_skew_ms` after the move, as the floor does while the node runs. One field, and one cursor save at each raise of the floor.

        **What it leaves** (D6's N1 and N2 on #641 at `94834c5a`). A heartbeat stall alone: more than `HeartbeatGapMax` in which the agent made no heartbeat (the `silent` cause), with a step back inside it while the node read no tick from the sensor (stopped, or with the link down, across a start). The floor then stops at the heartbeat before the stall, and a flow captured in it, held open past the first hello and written at its end ([S2], A6), is placed late by the step. A loss no longer leaves it, since a span's upper end holds the old clock's last reading (above). **AP-35's attacker is not known to be able to make a stall from outside the sensor host,** and that is an assumption about an agent not yet built (D6's N4 on #641 at `71fde2ac`, which corrects "cannot"): the agent makes a heartbeat every 10 s on its monotonic clock and never skips one because the link is down (CH-39, the heartbeat's cadence), but `HeartbeatGapMax` tolerates one late or missing heartbeat on a loaded sensor (CH-39), and whether a flood on the monitored network can delay the agent's heartbeats past 30 s is not shown. A leg of T-11 under load would show it. Otherwise a stall needs a fault of the agent or control of the sensor host, which can lie in any case (AP-36). **A run of unreadable entries leaves the same route** (D6's N5 on #641 at `71fde2ac`): CH-19 widens an unreadable entry's span only to the capture times of its readable neighbours, so where a corrupt stretch of more than about 22 s holds the step, the span's upper end is the last readable record before it. That is a fault of the sensor's disk, not something AP-35's attacker can cause from outside the host. **A stall is not always a gap the node records.** CH-39's third part is judged by `at_utc`, so a step back made inside the stall, of at least the stall's length less 30 s, brings two heartbeats within 30 s of each other and the chain does not see the stall; and the comparison at a start finds a shared step only where it exceeds the stop plus W's age (SL-16, "Across a restart of the node"). So a stall with a shared step inside it can be recorded as nothing, and the displacement is then bounded by the stop plus W's age; a larger step is found at the start and marks the `node-start` gap. It closes when [S2] is settled with the engine writing a flow's records at a bounded time.

        **With the sensor database lost, M is lost with it** (SL-31; D6's N5 on #641 at `94834c5a`). The floor at that start rests on what the agent resends and states: from the run file's copy of the run at `seq` 0, the records the agent still holds of that run, heartbeats included, and the stated spans, which raise it as above. A step back seen before the stop, which only M carried, is lost with M, and a capture before the stop is then placed late by it. With no copy held and no `taken` sent, the floor rests on no resent heartbeat, only on the spans the hello states. That needs two faults on the node, as SL-31's lost database does, and a re-arm before the dial (SL-30, SL-31), and status names the position as taken from the run file with the database lost.
      - **Why this form of D6's floor.** D6's floor is the highest `sensor_utc` since the baseline, plus the longest interval between two consecutive ticks' receipts since it, plus `max_skew_ms`. Both bound the old clock at the receipt that shows the move. This one does it with no interval: K placed the old clock against the node's monotonic clock at each tick, so its highest value, carried to the receipt, is the old clock's highest reading there. D6's interval is between receipts, while the moment of the move is bounded by the sending of the tick that shows it. Where the tick before the move was held in the stream longer than that tick, by more than `max_skew_ms` plus whatever the regime's longest interval exceeds the last one by, the receipts' interval falls short of the sending interval, and a capture just before the move can lie above D6's floor. With ticks on time that never happens, but a regime can be one tick long, after a held read loop (N2, below), and then nothing is left over.
      - **What it covers.** Every capture made before the move carries a time on the old clock no later than that clock's reading at the receipt that shows the move, so it is at or before the floor however late the engine writes it. That is every record the numbering rule at `50a67730` refused, and the case it missed: D6's case, a session captured while the source was 300 s ahead and written at its end (#645 §14 item 2, A6), numbered after the tick that showed the correction.
      - **What it costs.** After a step back of Δ, captures on the new clock fall at or before the floor for about Δ + `max_skew_ms` after the move, so their ties are refused, which fails closed and is aggregated. After a step forward nothing new is refused. **A clock set ahead and then corrected costs the whole excursion, across every start, with no bound** (D6's N3 on #641 at `94834c5a`): by fault (a sensor host whose hardware clock keeps local time east of UTC boots ahead until it syncs), by a wrong time source, or by AP-35's attacker, the floor rises to the top of the excursion, M carries it across starts, and every own session of that sensor then alerts under `clock-skew` until true time passes the floor: for a day after a day ahead. **This is a stated limit** (D1's ruling on D6's N3 on #641 at `94834c5a`): status shows the floor's value for each sensor beside the `clock-skew` counts, so the cause and its end can be read, and in C13 0.2 no one can lower it, so the cost is noise that fails closed and never a tie let through. **A sensor host that lies (AP-36) can set it so too, and it outlives the host** (D6's N3 on #641 at `71fde2ac`; an accepted limit for C13 0.2 by D1's ruling on #641, comment 6847521755, which D1 records in D4-92, not on `main` at `7d1be001`): one far-ahead `sensor_utc`, or one span stated far ahead, raises the floor, and it stays up after the host is rebuilt. **In C13 0.2 nothing ends it but true time passing the floor.** M is kept for the `sensor_id` across every enrolment, and is not part of the position that SL-R6 reads as none after a `rekey` or a `replace`, since a flow captured before an enrolment can be written in the next generation's run 1 (A6); so neither a rebuild nor a re-enrolment of any reason lowers it. The sensor database lost takes it with M (SL-31), which is a fault and not a remedy. A rule that starts a new floor at a new generation would need the start's rules applied to that generation's first hello; it is not proposed here, and is D1's to brief if the owner wants it.
      - **K moves with no move of either clock** (D6's N2 on #641 at `50a67730`, which corrects "K stays put" and "adds little" at `50a67730`). A read loop held past `max_skew_ms` (F-016's 3 s on the hosted Windows runner; #644 §1.5 item 6) delays one tick's receipt, so K falls by the hold: a change, which raises the floor to about the sensor's clock at that receipt, so the ties not yet decided then are refused. That K is not taken as the baseline (above), so K's return at the next tick is no change. Taken as the baseline, as at `e164e416`, the return was a second refusal, and a move of about the hold's length made just after the held tick showed no change at all, so captures between that receipt and the move were displaced by up to the hold plus `max_skew_ms`, unrefused (D6's N4 on #641 at `e164e416`). The raise is counted with the samples read after a held loop, as #644 §1.5 item 6 counts those, and not as a clock move. **On a Windows node K may drift** (an inference, not verified): Go's monotonic reading there is the interrupt time, which the time service's rate adjustment does not steer, so K drifts by the oscillator's error, about 2 s in 11 h at 50 ppm, and each crossing of `max_skew_ms` refuses the ties pending then. The first half's stored times are at most about 15 minutes old, so its drift is under 0.1 s. Each case fails closed, is aggregated and is shown in status.
      - **What it leaves** (AP-35). The bound assumes that the least delay of a tick in the stream since the baseline is within `max_skew_ms`: K is the offset less that delay, and the node cannot tell the two apart. A capture made before the move within that excess of the move can lie above the floor. It needs every tick since the baseline more than 2 s late in the stream, which is E's own residual (CH-22); wherever the two clocks agree, the alarm then stands. **Across a node start** the floor is carried by M, by the heartbeats read after the start up to the first current hello, and by the stated spans, and what is left there is stated with them (above). Records numbered before the first hello after a start are backlog of a first break, which counts as long, and take no tie (CH-39).
    - **A tie is decided at its record's read, with K, the floor, the alarm and the latest sample as they stand then; for a record a run's hold keeps, at the run's result. No tie is made while the offset of the node's wall clock from its monotonic clock has moved more than `max_skew_ms` since the receipt of the latest sample** (D6's closure for the node's side, in D6's N1 on #641 at `50a67730`: the latest sample was taken at a receipt on the node's wall clock, so it is a stored time too, and the first half applies to it). Its cost: ties decided between a step of the node's clock and the next sample are refused, which fails closed and is counted with the clock moves. A run that holds a record was dispatched before the record was read, so a step of the node's clock before the result lies between the run's `Dispatch` and its `Result`, and the first half refuses the window. **That rests on one run at a time** (D6's N3 on #641 at `e164e416`): the scheduler runs one run at a time (#644 §1.6). With two runs open at once, as a granted session under Phase 4 item 5 could make them, a record held by one run and decided at its result could tie to the other run's window, recorded after a move of the node's clock; whether the first half and this closure cover that case is owed with the change that lets two runs be open at once.
    - **No tie waits for a tick** (D3's ruling on D6's B1 on #644 at `bc19358e`, the choice D1 preferred). The wait D3 added at `e164e416` for D6's N1 on #641 at `50a67730` is withdrawn, and with it the tests `TestTieWaitsForTick` and `TestTieWaitHoldsDetector` (#644 §1.5). **Why:**
      - *The wait broke the order it had to keep.* A waiting late-index or open-table tie is committed after every record read while it waited. The detector's drive reads in `seq` order, and `judge` (`modules/detection/netdetect/own.go` l.526 on `main` at `7d1be001`) marks a session unseen once a later flow starts past its end plus `g`, so the session alerts `own-session-unseen` before its tie lands. Holding the drive only moves that alert to the end of the wait (D6's probe against `main` at `7d1be001`). For a run's hold, the run's `after_seq` cannot follow a release as written: `collection.run` takes `Result`'s `after_seq` before any tick can come.
      - *Every mechanism that would keep the order costs more than the residual is worth.* A deferral of judgement in `netdetect` is a change to D4's detector and its review. Making every record read after a waiting tie wait behind it holds the sensor's whole stream behind each record that would tie; the node's own collection makes such records at every run, so the stream would lag a tick at each run, and past `HoldRecordCap` (23) the hold would commit untied into false per-asset alerts. A run hold's later `afterSeq` needs a second value beside the one `collection.run` stores, or a scheduler that waits on the sensor (F4's family, against `HoldLockWait`'s 2 s). Each fails closed, but each adds a mechanism to close a window that needs the attacker to hold the sensor's clock, and each new mechanism has failed review once already.
      - *What is left is bounded and needs a precondition that is already a risk of its own,* below. The closure above removes the node's side for free, so a shared move of both clocks is refused once the node's clock has moved.
    - **What it leaves: the one-tick residual** (D6's N1 on #641 at `50a67730`, stated again; AP-35). A move of the sensor's clock not yet matched by the node's, or an eviction of records numbered before the tied one, made after the last tick the node took a sample from and before the record's read, shows only at the next such tick. A tie decided in that interval uses the old view: after a step back of the sensor's clock alone, a record captured after the step is placed with the stale latest sample and can tie to a session or run window the step's length earlier, and a span first stated in the next tick has not yet raised the floor.
      - **Its size:** one tick interval (10 s) for each move. It is longer while ticks are late or counted under `tick_early`. A sensor host that lies can arrange either, and such a host can lie about traffic in any case (AP-36). An honest sensor can make a tick early too (D6's N2 on #641 at `d83c07e4`): the node counts `tick_early` by arrival, a tick less than `TickMinSpacing` (5 s) after the last accepted one (CH-8), so a tick whose receipt is delayed by 5 s or more, by a held read loop on the node or by a delay on the management network (loss and retransmission, or a flood from the attacker who reaches that network for O-10), makes the next one early, and each early tick adds one tick interval. The window stays bounded by the ticks the node takes a sample from. The displacement is the move. Each such record ties at most one flow, and **the loss is the whole flow, not one alert** (D6's B1 on #641 at `d83c07e4`): a tied flow skips every base rule (`modules/detection/netdetect/detector.go` l.262 on `main` at `7d1be001`), and its later records tie through the open-flow table, so the flow raises no alert except `node-address-spoofed`, which has no tie check (`own.go` l.177) and judges a flow from the node's address sent on one of the node's own segments from a MAC that is not the node's. For that flow this is AP-35's threat itself, until the owed mitigation below narrows it to read-only flows.
      - **What it takes:** control of the sensor's clock alone, through its time source (O-10: from the management network, where the source is unauthenticated) or the sensor host itself (AP-36); a move timed into that interval; and a flow from the node's IP address and, on the node's own segments, one of the node's MACs (from any other MAC there, `node-address-spoofed` raises; off those segments it does not judge the flow), to a relay whose session or run window lies the move's length earlier. That is a capable insider on the station network who also controls the site's time source (D1's ruling on #641, comment 6848042423). A move of a source that serves both hosts is refused by the closure above once the node's clock has moved too, and ties only while the node's clock has not yet followed. **The eviction branch** (D6's N3 on #641 at `d83c07e4`) matters only across a node start, since while the node runs K shows every move at the next tick; it needs a step during a stop or an outage that no tick showed, the buffer full during the drain so that the heartbeats around the step are evicted after the first current hello and before the tied record's read (CH-20; traffic on the monitored network suffices), and a record the engine writes late ([S2]).
      - **What shows:** a move left standing is seen at the next tick, which raises the floor and, past `max_skew_ms`, the alarm, so later ties are refused and the cause is in status; the tie made before it stands. A move made and undone inside the interval is seen by no tick.
    - **The owed mitigation** (D1's ruling on #641, comment 6848042423, which D1 records in D4-92 as owed; D4's detector change, in the third part's implementation briefs, before item 4's gate; D6 tests it with a control, a detector that skips every base rule on a tied flow, which raises nothing): the platform never writes to a device (P2), so a flow tied to a platform session that carries any write or control function code still raises `unauthorized-write` (or its successor rule); the tie suppresses own-session alerts for reads only. Until it is built, the residual can hide a whole flow, writes included; once it is, the residual is narrowed to read-only flows.
    - **CH-22 refuses a tie for three causes** (D3's ruling on `records-auditor`'s audit of the three heads, which found "two" and "three" in #644 and #645): the alarm stands; the latest sample is outside `max_skew_ms`; or the rule on the two clocks refuses it (this note's two halves, the closure for the node's side included). The first two are standing states, under which every observation of the window is marked; the third refuses record by record. A text that counts the causes says three; a text about the standing states alone names them as CH-22's two standing causes, and does not say the shift exists only then, since the one-tick residual (above) shifts a record with neither standing.
    - **What is refused alerts.** A backlog record refused at the break's ends is judged by CH-39, under `sensor-records-late`, **and a record that the break's ends and another rule here both refuse is judged by CH-39 too,** with `node.backlog: true` and no `node.skewed` (D6's N5 on #641 at `50a67730`). Any other flow refused a tie under either half is committed untied with `node.skewed: true`, and a run whose window is refused is marked `skewed`, so their own-session alerts come under the `clock-skew` key (CH-41), which then means "no tie was made for a clock, the sensor's or the node's". Status counts this cause apart from the alarm's and the latest sample's, a node start apart again, and a floor raised by a stated span apart again (D6's N2 on #641 at `71fde2ac`; the names are D5's). A flow that matches no session keeps its own key (#644 §1.5 item 4).
      - **A run committed before the move cannot be marked** (D6's N3 on #641 at `50a67730`): the store is append-only. Its window is refused all the same, so its own session's flow alerts under `clock-skew`, aggregated, but the run's own `own-session-unseen` comes per asset, not aggregated. It is bounded by the late index's 15 minutes before the move, and it is noise, never concealment.
    - **It fails closed.** With no move, the offsets agree, K stays within `max_skew_ms` of its baseline but for the cases of N2 above, and nothing changes. After a move, the node's own sessions recorded before it, and the records captured before it whose tie is decided after it, alert, aggregated. A step is named by `time.node-stepped` where the node's check finds it, and by the counts in status.
    - **What it takes, and what it leaves** (AP-35). To use this route an attacker needs control of a time source that serves both hosts (O-10: from the management network where it is unauthenticated), or of the node host. What stays: a displacement within `max_skew_ms`; the one-tick residual (above): a move of the sensor's clock, or an eviction, made after the last tick sampled and before a record's read, for a tie decided in that interval, undone or not; the floor's slack, which needs every tick since the baseline more than `max_skew_ms` late in the stream (above); and across a node start, a step back inside a heartbeat stall of more than `HeartbeatGapMax` while the node read no tick, against a flow captured in that stretch, held open past the first hello and written at its end ([S2], A6; the floor across a node start, above). A move of the node's clock made and undone is not left: each stored time keeps the offset it was recorded with, read at the time and not at a check, so a time recorded while the clock stood moved is refused once it is put back.
    - *Tests* (on the test clock, ticks every 10 s and `tie_grace_s` 30 s unless a case says otherwise; each "dial that fails" puts its session in the late index; names are D5's):
      - **First half, a step both clocks share** (D6's case, reworked for D6's N1 on #641 at `e164e416`: with the step 20 s after the dial, the capture floor refused the session too, so a node with no first half did not tie it either): `tie_grace_s` 60 s; a dial to a relay that fails, recorded as [10:00:00, 10:00:10] on the node's clock; at 10:00:12 the test source's clock and the node's are stepped back 300 s together; at 10:05:50, true time, a Telnet session from the node's address to that relay, stamped about 10:00:50 by the source, inside the window widened by 60 s and past the capture floor, which the step raises to at most about 10:00:22.5 (D6's desk simulation over tick phases). It is committed untied with `node.skewed: true`, its `own-session-outside-record` alert under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node with no first half compares `t_sensor + E` with the stored window as it is, and ties it to the run.
      - **First half, the node's clock corrected** (reworked for D5's point on #641 at `50a67730`: at `50a67730` the window was recorded while `sensor-clock-skew` stood, so no window was stored for a tie, and the control tied nothing either). `tie_grace_s` 60 s. The node's clock and the source's both 300 s ahead of true time, so they agree and no alarm stands; a dial to the relay that fails, recorded as [X − 10 s, X] on the node's clock; 2 s after X the node's clock alone is stepped back 300 s, which raises the alarm; 2 s later the source's clock is stepped back 300 s too, and the alarm clears once E's moved samples leave the six ticks; 340 s after the dial's end, true time, a Telnet session from the node's address to that relay, stamped and placed at about X + 40 s, inside the window widened by `tie_grace_s`, and after the capture floor that the source's step raised, at about X + 16 s. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node with no first half ties it. **The same correction made on both clocks together in steps of 1,900 ms, one every 2 s of monotonic time, from 2 s after X,** so that the alarm never rises, **with the test clock running the step check every second, so that no check spans two steps** (D6's N5 on #641 at `50a67730`: a check 10 s apart sees five steps, 9.5 s, and finds them); the session 345 s after the dial's end, placed at about X + 45 s, once the correction is done and past the capture floor, which the steps raise to about X + 29 s at most (D6's desk simulation over seven tick phases, X + 27.6 s to X + 29.0 s; D6's N2 on #641 at `e164e416`): untied. *Control:* a node that compares only the steps its check finds, each more than `max_skew_ms`, finds none and ties it.
      - **First half, a time recorded before the node's start** (reworked so that the capture floor across a start does not refuse it first: with both clocks stepped back during the stop, as at `e164e416`, the session fell below the start's floor, and its control tied nothing): `tie_grace_s` 600 s; both clocks true; a dial to the relay that failed and ended 30 s before the node stops for one minute, the sensor running; after the start, 60 s after the first hello, once the backlog is through, a Telnet session from the node's address to that relay, placed inside that session's window widened by 600 s and past the start's floor. Committed untied. *Control:* a node that ties by the late index as it rebuilds it at a start ties it.
      - **Second half, a sensor clock corrected, a record in flight:** the source's clock 300 s ahead of the node's, `sensor-clock-skew` standing; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at T, true time, stamped T + 300 s, **its `start` record numbered and synced before the first tick after the correction** (D6's N5 on #641 at `50a67730`); the source's clock stepped back 300 s at T + 5 s, and that record sent at T + 305 s behind a queue of records, ticks on time; a dial to that relay that fails at T + 300 s for 2 s, before the record arrives. The record is placed at T + 300 s, inside the run's window, and is committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node with CH-22 and the first half alone ties it, since no step of the node's clock lies between the window and now, and the two clocks agree when the record arrives.
      - **Second half, both clocks stepped together:** the same, with both clocks true until T + 5 s and then stepped back 300 s together, the session stamped T, and the dial at T + 300 s, true time, which is T on the new clocks. The record is placed at T and committed untied. *Control:* the same node ties it.
      - **Second half, a record written late** (D6's B1 on #641 at `50a67730`, D6's test): the first of these two cases, with the session held open until after the dial, and the engine writing its `start` record only at the session's end, so the record is numbered after the first tick that shows the correction. Placed at T + 300 s, inside the run's window: committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node that refuses by numbering, no record numbered at or before the `newest_seq` of the tick that shows the move (the second half at `50a67730`), ties it.
      - **Second half, a move inside a break:** the source's clock 300 s ahead, `sensor-clock-skew` standing; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at T, stamped T + 300 s and held open; the connection reset at T + 10 s, the source's clock stepped back 300 s at T + 20 s, and the next hello accepted at T + 40 s, so the alarm clears on the new connection; a dial to that relay that fails at T + 300 s for 2 s; the session closed after the dial, and the engine writing its `start` record at its end, numbered after the hello's `newest_seq` and read once the backlog is through. Placed at T + 300 s: committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node that takes K's baseline afresh at each hello, which sees no change and ties it.
      - **Second half, a K read after a held loop** (D6's N4 on #641 at `e164e416`): both clocks true and K at its baseline, `tie_grace_s` 3 s; a tick sent at R − 3 s and received at R after the node's read loop is held 3 s, which raises the floor to about R + 2 s; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at R + 5 s, stamped R + 5 s and held open; both clocks stepped back 1.5 s at R + 5.5 s and again at R + 6.5 s, the test clock running the step check every second, so that the check finds neither step and E does not move; the next tick sent at R + 7 s and received on time; at R + 9 s, true time, a dial to that relay that fails for 2 s, recorded as [R + 6 s, R + 8 s] on the new clock; the session closed after it, its `start` record read after the dial and placed at R + 5 s, inside the window widened by 3 s. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`: the tick at R + 7 s shows K 3 s from the baseline, and the floor rises to about R + 9 s. *Control:* a node that takes the K read after the held loop as the baseline, which sees no change at R + 7 s and ties it.
      - **Second half, the floor never falls** (D6's N5 on #641 at `e164e416`): the record written late (above), with the node's read loop held 3 s at a tick after the correction and before the record is read. The held tick shows a change whose raise, on the new clock, is below the floor the correction raised, so the floor stays. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node that sets the floor to each raise's value, whose floor falls to the new clock's reading, which ties it.
      - **Across a node start, a step before the stop** (D6's B1 on #641 at `e164e416`, D6's test): both clocks true; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at T, stamped T and held open; at T + 10 s a source serving both hosts steps both clocks back 600 s, which the node sees at the next tick, raising the floor to at most about T + 22 s and saving it at once; at T + 60 s, true time, the node stops, and it starts one minute later; at T + 600 s, true time, which is T on the new clocks, a dial to that relay that fails; the session closed after it, its `start` record written at its end and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node with no M, the floor held in memory only, which ties it; the first break's heartbeats are all on the new clock, so their raise does not reach T. *Second control* (D6's N4 on #641 at `94834c5a`; the start bound named for D6's N6 on #641 at `71fde2ac`): a node that applies the step rule to the capture floor, M and the start bound, moving them back by the step it finds and capping each at the node's wall clock at the `node-start` gap's close, which ties it. A node that moves or caps the capture floor and M alone still refuses the session, since the start bound, taken from the saved M at the start, is about T + 54 s.
      - **Across a node start, a step during the stop** (D6's test): both clocks true; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at T, stamped T and held open; the node stopped at T + 20 s for one minute, the sensor running, and both clocks stepped back 300 s during the stop; after the start, a dial to that relay that fails at T on the new clocks; the session's `start` record written at its end, after the hello, and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node that starts with no floor, no M and no raise from the first break's heartbeats, which ties it. *Second control* (D6's N4 on #641 at `94834c5a`; the moment named for D6's N5 on #641 at `0ed3138c`): a node that applies the step rule to the capture floor, M and the start bound, capping each at the node's wall clock at the `node-start` gap's close, which ties it.
      - **Across a node start, a capture during the stop:** both clocks true; the node stopped for ten minutes from S, the sensor running; at S + 5 min a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay, stamped S + 5 min and held open; at S + 6 min both clocks stepped back 600 s; the node started at S + 10 min; at S + 15 min, true time, which is S + 5 min on the new clocks, a dial to that relay that fails; the session closed after it, its `start` record written at its end and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node with M but no raise from the first break's heartbeats, whose start bound is about S + 32 s at most, which ties it.
      - **Across a node start, a new run during the stop** (D6's B1 on #641 at `94834c5a`, D6's test): the previous case's stop, with the sensor's agent restarted at S + 1 min, so that a new run begins while the old run still holds records above `taken`; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at S + 3 min, stamped S + 3 min and held open; both clocks stepped back 600 s at S + 6 min; the node started at S + 10 min, its first hello the old run's, with `current: false`, whose heartbeats end before S + 1 min, and the new run's hello following the drain; at S + 13 min, true time, which is S + 3 min on the new clocks, a dial to that relay that fails; the session's `start` record written at its end, after the new run's hello, and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`, the floor at about S + 382 s (D6's desk model). *Control:* a node that raises the floor only from the heartbeats of the first hello's run, whose floor reaches about S + 82 s and whose start bound is about S + 32 s, which ties it.
      - **Across a node start, a step inside a lost span** (D6's N1 on #641 at `94834c5a`): both clocks true; the node stopped for ten minutes from S, the sensor running; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at S + 3 min, stamped S + 3 min and held open; the test-only C13 source evicts every record made from S + 2 min to S + 7 min, true time, heartbeats included, as a full buffer does, and states that span with the rise in `drops_channel` that pays for it (CH-40); both clocks stepped back 900 s at S + 6 min, so that the span's upper end is the old clock's last reading, about S + 360 s; the node started at S + 10 min; at S + 18 min, true time, which is S + 3 min on the new clocks, a dial to that relay that fails; the session's `start` record written at its end and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`, the floor at about S + 392 s. *Control:* a node that raises the floor from the heartbeats read after the start but not from stated spans, whose floor reaches about S + 152 s from the last heartbeat before the span (the heartbeats after the step lie below S − 260 s), which ties it.
      - **Across a node start, a span first stated after the first current hello** (D6's N7 on #641 at `71fde2ac`): both clocks true; the node stopped for ten minutes from S, the sensor running; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at S + 3 min, stamped S + 3 min and held open; both clocks stepped back 900 s at S + 6 min; the node started at S + 10 min, and its first hello, with `current` true, states no span; while the node drains the backlog, once it has taken every record made before S + 2 min, the test-only C13 source evicts every record made from S + 2 min to S + 7 min, true time, heartbeats included, none of them yet sent, as a full buffer does during a drain, and states that span first in the next tick, with the rise in `drops_channel` that pays for it (CH-40); at S + 18 min, true time, which is S + 3 min on the new clocks, a dial to that relay that fails; the session's `start` record written at its end and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`, the floor at about S + 392 s. *Control:* a node that raises the floor only from spans stated up to the first current hello, whose floor reaches about S + 152 s from the last heartbeat before the span, which ties it.
      - **Across a node start, a step before a `rekey`** (D6's N2 on #641 at `0ed3138c`): the seventh case's session, step and saved floor; at T + 30 s a `rekey` of that sensor applied, under which no hello is yet accepted; the node stopped at T + 60 s, true time, and started one minute later; the new generation's run 1 the first run read after the start; at T + 600 s, true time, a dial to that relay that fails; the session's `start` record written at its end in that run 1 (A6) and read once the backlog is through. Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node that reads M as none with the lower generation's position (SL-R6), which ties it.
      - **The node's side of a tie decided at read** (D6's closure in D6's N1 on #641 at `50a67730`; D3's ruling on D6's B1 on #644 at `bc19358e`): the two clocks in agreement; a Telnet session from the node's address to a relay at T, stamped T and held open; at T + 297 s, 3 s after a tick's receipt, the node's clock alone stepped back 300 s; at T + 298 s, true time, a dial to that relay that fails for 2 s, recorded as [T − 2 s, T] on the new clock; the session closed at T + 299 s, its `start` record written at its end and read before the next tick, placed at T with the latest sample from before the step; the source sending no heartbeat and the test clock running no node tick between the step and the tie's decision (D6's N1 on #641 at `d83c07e4`; as the eighth gap case says). Committed untied, under `clock-skew`. *Control:* a node without the closure, which ties it; the stored window was recorded after the step, so the first half alone passes it, and the sensor's clock did not move, so the floor does not refuse it. Were a step check to run in that interval, it would move the latest sample outside `max_skew_ms`, and both causes would hold; the refusal is then counted once, under the standing cause, `skew_latest_windows`, as a node without the closure counts it, so the count does not hang on the closure; `skew_moved_windows` takes a refusal by the rule on the two clocks only where neither standing cause holds.
      - **Which count takes a refusal** (D6's N5 on #644 at `bc19358e`; `records-auditor`'s audit of the three heads): in every case above committed untied under `clock-skew`, the test also asserts the status count that took it, under #644 §1.5 item 5's names (D5's): `skew_span_windows` for a floor last raised by a stated span (the cases of a lost span and of a span first stated after the first current hello), `skew_prestart_windows` for a time recorded before the node's start, or a refusal by M, the start bound or the first break's heartbeats (the cases across a node start, the `rekey` case included), and `skew_moved_windows` for a move of either clock seen while the node ran (the closure's case included); and that status shows the sensor's capture floor at its value after the case. *Control:* a node that counts every refusal as a clock move, which fails the first two; and a node that shows no floor, which fails each.
      - **`TestShortBreakNodeStepNoTie`,** first case: the previous bullet's test. **Second case, D5's:** a 40 s break with no step in it, `tie_grace_s` 1 s; 1 s after the hello the source's clock and the node's are stepped back 30 s together, and the backlog is read for 90 s; a dial to the relay that fails, 24 s after the step for 2 s; a backlog record made 35 s into the break, of a flow from the node's address to that relay, stamped 35 s, read 70 s after the step. It is committed untied with `node.backlog: true` under `sensor-records-late`, **and with no `node.skewed`** (D6's N5 on #641 at `50a67730`). *Control:* a node with no rule on the two clocks ties it, since the break's two lengths agree and the record falls inside the break's ends on the old clock. **In both cases the capture floor refuses the record too** (in the first, the hello shows the step; in the second, the tick after it), so each pins the break's ends through the key: a node that lacks the rule on the break's ends commits the record with `node.skewed: true` under `clock-skew`, and fails the case.
  - **The sign.** The offset is the node's time minus the sensor's, so a sensor time is brought to the node's clock by adding E. D3's first text on #627 said "less E", which was wrong by twice the offset (D4 found it; D4-87).
  - **"Late" is the sensor's own word** and carries no trust. The node's own measure is the break.
  - **What stays** is in AP-35: the one-tick residual, a move of the sensor's clock or an eviction shown only at the first tick after a record's read, for a tie decided before that tick, undone or not; a move of the sensor's clock made and undone inside a short break, for the records captured while it stood; a displacement within `max_skew_ms`; the capture floor's slack; and, across a node start, a step back inside a heartbeat stall of more than `HeartbeatGapMax` while the node read no tick, against a flow captured in it and written after the first hello (the rule on the two clocks, above). The node's clock moved and restored, inside a short break or between two of its checks, ties nothing it recorded while moved (the rule on the two clocks, above; D6's B1 on #641 at `c0cf1ecc`).
- *Answers:* AP-35. A sensor whose clock is wrong, by fault or on purpose, moves observations into or out of a session's window.
- *Tests:* the cases of the short-break bullet and the rule on the two clocks, above, which the two documents carry; and as named there: C13 0.2's T-2, T-7 and T-8 (§15 there), and the node's `TestSkewStepBackTiesNothing` and `TestSkewCorrectionAddsE` (#628 §1.5), with the gate's skew-alarm line.

## 6. Revocation

**SL-R1. `node.sensor_revoke` is admin-signed and names the `sensor_id`, its generation and pin, a reason, and optionally `suspect_from`:** the time from which the administrator no longer trusts what the sensor sent. Absent, it means from enrolment.

**SL-R2. It closes the link at once, before the node's own record.** D1 kept this order as the fail-closed one (ruling on #624). Once the collector has accepted the request, the node saves the revoked state, closes the connection and stops dialling, and then writes C8 `sensor.revoked`. For every other admin decision the node's record comes first, and a failed write stops the decision (D4-68 ruling 9). Here that order would keep a revoked sensor trusted, which is failing open.

- **Audit before act still holds.** D1-11 (`docs/phase1/A1-decisions.md`) is the rule that collection which uses a credential stops when its audit record cannot be written. SL-R2 does not break it: a revocation uses no credential, and the collector's decision is in the audit chain, as `collector.request`, before the node acts on it (D5 on #624).
- **If the node's own write fails,** the link stays closed, status shows the record as owed, and the node writes it once when the chain accepts writes again.
- **If the revoked state cannot be saved,** the dialer for that sensor stays off for the rest of the run, and at its next start the node derives the state again from the decision in the chain.
- **What a revocation still needs.** It waits for the collector, and it cannot be recorded while the audit store is full. The protective path that needs neither is the local one: an edited or removed `network_sensor` entry stops the dial (SL-9). That revokes nothing and marks nothing, and the signed revocation follows when it can be made.
- **How soon "at once" is** (D1's rulings 2 and 3 on #628; #628 §1.3). From the node's fold of the collector's acceptance to the close: within one second. From the signature to the close: about 15 s when no run is in progress, about 615 s at worst when the collector answers, and **open while the collector holds requests.** The relay stays where it is: a second client on the collector's pipe is a change to the privileged process that this gain does not justify. Every record committed in that window is at or below `through_seq` and after `suspect_from`, so it is marked (SL-R3).
- **Because that bound can be open, a pending request stops the dial.** Every 15 s the node reads its admin directory. While a `node.sensor_revoke` request that names a live sensor is undecided, the node stops dialling that sensor and closes its connection, with an alarm. It reads the request for its `sensor_id` and pin only, before any signature is checked, so this path can only stop a link. It lifts if the collector refuses the request, if the file goes away, or when the request's own expiry passes. **What it costs:** whoever can write the node's admin directory can hold a sensor's link down, with the alarm standing. That directory is under the protected-file rule, and writing it is already a compromise of the node (SL-5).
- **No state of the node holds a revocation up** (D5 on #631, N1). The node's design bound both kinds to its install key, so that a revocation waited while a collector reset was open and was refused for a retired install key. A revocation can only stop a link. So in those states the node acts as the pending path does: the pin is held and the link closed, with the alarm, and `sensor.revoked` is written once the install key is trusted again. An enrolment stays bound to the install key.

**SL-R3. Records already stored are kept, and marked.** Nothing is deleted: they are evidence. The store is append-only, so the mark is not written into them, and it needs no rewrite: a reader applies it from each record's commit time and the `through_seq` (D5 on #624). `sensor.revoked` carries, for each of the sensor's streams, the last committed `seq` (`through_seq`), with `suspect_from`. Every reader (status, an export, a screen later) shows that sensor's records at or below `through_seq` and committed at or after `suspect_from` as coming from a revoked sensor. The node also commits a health event to the health stream, not to the sensor's alert stream (D5 on #624). Its name is `sensor.revocation-applied`, so that it does not share the audit action's name (ruling 4 on #631), so an upstream collector sees the revocation in a stream it already receives. **Records held at intake when the revocation lands are committed before `through_seq` is taken,** or they would land above it unmarked. None is dropped silently.

- **The order at the node** (#628 §1.3; D3's L3 on #628). `through_seq` is taken only after the connection's read loop has returned, so no record is in commit. The alert stream's `through_seq` is taken once the detector has judged every observation at or below the observation stream's, so that no alert raised later from the sensor's earlier observations lands above it unmarked. `sensor.revoked` is owed until then, and may be written late (§11.2, C8-3).
- **A discarded buffer is declared.** After a revocation the sensor's old key no longer passes the handshake, so what its buffer held cannot be sent. The next enrolment's first hello declares it, with its count and its spans (CH-12; D1's ruling 5 on #627).

**SL-R4. Later records are refused.** The node never dials a revoked pin again. A revoked pin can never be enrolled again, under any `sensor_id`. **A verified revocation is never dropped** (D5's B1 on #631; ruling 3 on #631): it always adds its pin to the node's revoked pins, whatever the node holds for it. That covers an enrolment that is still pending, the pin of an older generation, and a node whose sensor state is unknown or unread (§11.2, C8-3). The next enrolment of that `sensor_id` carries a higher generation and another key.

**SL-R5. The network baseline is acknowledged again.** From `sensor.revoked`, the acknowledgement of that sensor's baseline is void. After a new enrolment, the rules that need an acknowledged baseline do not run for that sensor until a person signs `node.network_baseline_ack` again. Whether they sign the old document or learn a new one is their judgement, and the administrator's client must show whether the learning period overlaps the suspect period. While the acknowledgement is void and the sensor is feeding, the node shows a standing alarm, so the weaker detection is never silent. No administrator's client exists yet, so the overlap view is a requirement on one (as HR-14 is). **This rule also rests on something that is not built:** the node does not yet apply `node.network_baseline_ack` (D5 on #624; at `1f5b307f` no file under `platform/` or `cmd/` outside the tests names `netack`).

**SL-R6. A re-key with no compromise is not a revocation.** A new enrolment of the same `sensor_id`, with reason `rekey` and a new key, replaces the old pin at once (SL-6), and the acknowledgement stands. That gives the enrolment grant nothing new: a sensor can lie about traffic under any baseline, and a baseline can only be widened by `node.network_baseline_ack`, which is another grant (SL-7).

- **The reason is checked against the pin** (§11.1, C7-4; D3's condition on D1's ruling 5 on #627). `renew` keeps the key, and `rekey` and `replace` change it; any other pairing is `denied`. The node decides what carries over from the pin being unchanged **and** the reason `renew`, never from the reason alone.
- **A `renew` loses nothing of the key it keeps.** The sensor's buffer, its run counter and the node's position carry over to the new generation, as long as that position began at or after the generation of the pin the `renew` keeps (next bullet), so an expired enrolment, renewed, flushes what it held. Any other reason starts run 1 of the new generation, and what the sensor discards is declared (CH-12).
- **What carries over is a position of the key the renewal keeps, and nothing older** (P4-4-L6; D5's B4 on #634). The node keeps, for each `sensor_id`, the generation at which its position began: set by a `new`, a `rekey` and a `replace`, and kept by a `renew` with the pin unchanged. It can be folded from the `sensor.enrolled` records, as the pins are (SL-6). **A saved position of a lower generation reads as none:** `node-hello` carries no `taken`, and CH-13 row 3 reads the counter as 0. **M is not part of that position** (SL-17; D6's N2 on #641 at `0ed3138c`): the capture floor's M is kept for the `sensor_id` across every enrolment, whatever its reason, since a flow captured before an enrolment can be written in the next generation's run 1 (A6). The case is SL-17's "a step before a `rekey`".
  - *Why.* Without it, a `rekey` under which no hello was accepted, followed by a `renew`, carries the older key's position over. The first hello is then closed as `run-regressed`, which writes a replay record that is false, and the new pair never earns SL-30's proof, so the link stops at the dial limit until another `rekey`.
  - **It is never done by writing a reset cursor at the enrolment** (D4-90). Under SL-30 a cursor of the live generation proves the pair, and that pair has not answered.
  - *Test:* SL-T10's carry-over leg.
- **Applying any enrolment closes that sensor's open connection first,** so the old pin is replaced at once (SL-6; #628 §1.3). **This holds for every reason, a `renew` with the pin unchanged included** (D4's B1 on #633; D1 adopted it in D4-90, and D3 confirms it). Each connection's hello is judged against the enrolment in force (SL-12, SL-13), and a renewal changes the generation and the `not_after` that the open connection was judged against. One rule with no branch on the reason is also the one with fewer ways to be built wrong. Its cost: after a `renew`, the node's redials close as `binding` until the sensor's end holds the renewal (SL-5 step 6). The pair keeps its proof (SL-30), and the interval is a gap (SL-16).

**SL-R7. Nothing is sent to the sensor.** The link carries no revoke command (SL-19). The node stops trusting, which is all that safety needs. What is done with the machine is a site procedure (O-4). The close for a revocation or a replaced enrolment is a TLS close with no C13 message, and after the decision the node takes no further record of that connection and sends no acknowledgement (CH-37).

**SL-R8. If the node's own link key is lost or replaced,** every sensor of that node is enrolled again at its console, at the next generation.

- *Answers:* a compromised or stolen sensor host (AP-34 (a), HR-21); a baseline learned through a sensor that was already false (AP-36 (a)).
- *Tests:* SL-T10; SL-T12 for SL-R7; and SL-T20 for SL-R8.

## 7. Direction, and what the link carries

**SL-18. The node opens the link and gains no inbound port.** It dials each sensor as it dials its forward targets, and its firewall allows that one destination. The link agent's listener is bound to the sensor host's management address only: never a wildcard address, and never the monitoring interface, which has no address. It is closed until the sensor is enrolled (SL-4).

- *Answers:* AP-34 (1). Nothing on the station LAN can reach the channel, and enabling a sensor adds no way into the node.
- *Tests:* SL-T11, SL-T7.

**SL-19. The link carries the sensor's C13 records and the node's acknowledgements, and nothing else.**

- Sensor to node: the hello, ticks, heartbeats and observations.
- **`tick` is new in C13 0.2, and D3 accepts it as a fourth type from the sensor** (CH-8; D3 on #627, note 1). It is decoded strictly, carries no text and no frame bytes, is never stored, and is made when it is sent, so it is the node's one live reading of the sensor's clock (SL-17). What reaches the sensor is unchanged: two types. A tick under another `sensor_id` counts as SL-10's refusal does, and one that comes faster than the interval is counted and does no further work.
- Node to sensor: the hello and acknowledgements.
- Each end decodes strictly from a closed set, and treats the other as hostile input. The node's messages to the sensor are small and bounded, and carry no text.
- **An acknowledgement's only effect is to move the agent's trim cursor,** as TB5 limits a RELP response to moving the forwarding cursor. An acknowledgement for another run, or above the highest `seq` sent, is counted and closes the connection. C13 0.2 states the bound as the larger of the hello's first `seq` less one and the highest `seq` sent on the connection (CH-9).
- **The position in the node's hello is bounded the same way.** One that names a run the agent does not hold, or a `seq` above that run's newest, is not applied and is counted, and the agent sends its true hello for the node to judge (CH-6; D3's B2 on #627).
- **No command and no configuration reaches the sensor over the link:** no rule or baseline, no capture filter, no time, no update, no restart, no request for logs or ring files. The link agent has no code path from a link message to anything but the trim cursor.
- *Answers:* AP-7's shape, one hop down. A compromised node that could configure its sensors could switch off redaction, load a rule that transmits (AP-32), or use them as a foothold beside the station LAN. With this rule a compromised node gains nothing on the sensor host, and a compromised sensor gains nothing on the node beyond lying (AP-34 (b)).
- *Consequence.* The sensor's settings change only through the signed update (SL-25) or at the console (SL-23). The node learns them from the coverage statement, and `sensor-coverage-mismatch` is its check.
- *The configuration digest.* It was asked of C13 0.2 on #624, and C13 0.2 gives it as `config_digest`, in the hello and in the coverage statement (CH-32): a digest over each program's hash, every configuration file the programs read at start, the settings, and the state of each redaction slot. A local edit, such as an allow-list widened, then shows at the node as a digest it does not expect. A redaction slot can be off only in a test build (SL-22). Like the rest of the coverage statement it is the sensor's own word, so it shows an edit and not a compromise (AP-36). The agent hashes the files on disk at the run's start, and not what each program loaded (D3 on #627, note 12). What SL-3's new shape asks of its list is in §13.
- *Test:* SL-T12.

**SL-20. A sensor that is cut, blind or flooding must never stop SER or Syslog collection.** Nor may any fault in a sensor's start, sizing or paperwork fail the node's start (SL-9). The separate store classes and quotas exist (C5 0.9). No flood over a network link has run (D3 on #618). Under option (b) the dialer and the reader add their own bounds: the frame cap (64 KiB, C13), a read deadline, bounded memory for each link, a redial back-off, and a floor between immediate redials (10 s; CH-38), so that a sensor which closes cleanly after every hello cannot drive the dial rate (D3's B1 on #627). P4-4-L3 item 8 names the test.

What the node's design must keep (D5 on #624, and the reviews of #628):

- a missing or mismatched enrolment is an alarm, never a failure at start (SL-9), and so is every other fault of a sensor's start: its intake, its database and its sizing;
- sensor work stays off the scheduler's tick, or is bounded there, so a slow link cannot delay collection;
- `sensor.link_refused` stays capped by time (§11.2), because a full audit store stops credentialed collection (D1-11, V-S9, `TestAuditFullSendsNothing`);
- **the platform class keeps collection's own need.** A sensor's health events go to the class that holds `collection.missed`, and that class refuses new records when it is full. So they are capped by time, and written only while collection's reserve is left (#628 §1.9 item 5);
- a cap that protects collection must not quietly switch off the own-session check: a session whose tie could not be made is kept, marked and judged, with an alarm (#628 §1.9 item 4; AP-36 (2));
- a sensor's faults stay inside the sensor's own database.

**The couplings found on `main`** (D5 and D6 on #628; `docs/phase4/D5-sensor-node.md` §1.0). There are ten paths, F1 to F10, by which a sensor's work on the node can reach SER collection, Syslog receive, forwarding or the node's start. **An eleventh, F11, joined them by ruling 1 on #631.** **Each is `desk`:** read from the code on `main` at `551b03dd` by D5 and D6, checked by `records-auditor`, and **not reproduced by a run.** D3's reviews of #628 read the code for them and agreed. D3 did not derive them again for this change. F11 is D3's own, read in `modules/security/nodekey/register.go` on `main`, and confirmed there by D6 and D5 on #631.

| | What it does | What it needs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | A full `platform` class holds collection runs, and a sensor's health events are written to that class | A feeding sensor |
| F2 | A fault in the intake's start fails the node's start | **The section alone** |
| F3 | The sensor's work, with a detector drive that has no bound, runs inside the node's tick | A feeding sensor |
| F4 | The scheduler's run path waits on the link's lock | A feeding sensor |
| F5 | The tick holds the state lock across a database's resume, and every store call of collection waits on that lock | **The section alone** |
| F6 | One prune mutex covers every database, and collection takes it on the run path | **The section alone** |
| F7 | A database that opens and then fails a read fails the node's start | **The section alone** |
| F8 | A required audit consumer whose cursor stops holds the audit stream. It is a property of the design in #628's first text, and not of `main` | That design |
| F9 | A mode change of any database writes an audit record and a health event, with no cap | **The section alone** |
